### [1] [The noise cost of reservoir locality in broadband quantum amplifier chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20654)
*Chengxi Li,Hao Zhu,Tie-Fu Zhang,Lin Zhuang,Wanzi Sun,Wuming Liu*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本文研究定向量子放大器中储层噪声对灵敏度的限制问题,通过构建邻位损耗通道并在局域兼容条件下达到已知单频最小值,比较了不同链结构的噪声性能,为低温微波阵列的储层设计和噪声校准提供理论指导。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 定向量子放大器在实现弱信号读出的同时保护信号源,但储层噪声会限制其灵敏度。有限带宽抑制必须在频率依赖响应、固定局域耦合和有限速率之间进行权衡。现有研究缺乏对多站点链结构中噪声性能的系统分析,需要建立理论基准并为实际低温微波阵列设计提供指导。

Method: 1) 在局域兼容条件下构建邻位损耗通道,以已知的单频最小值作为无上限基准;2) 对指定的均匀链和二聚化链,比较在匹配漂移、端口、高增益带和完整增益/损耗率上限条件下的固定储层性能;3) 利用边界响应界限给出必要的速率和需要二阶邻近的目标;4) 在匹配总耦合预算条件下进行有限辅助计算,量化噪声-分辨率容差。

Result: 在代表性参数cap R=8和半宽Ω=0.05下,对于所有有限N≥64的均匀链,最近邻算术带平均噪声超过无限制最优值1.01265个量子以上。边界响应界限确定了所需的速率和需要二阶邻近范围的目标。在N=64时,有限辅助计算在匹配总耦合预算下仍保持噪声优势,响应界限量化了噪声-分辨率容差。

Conclusion: 研究为设计带宽100 kHz的低温微波阵列提供了理论依据,明确了储层设计和噪声校准的关键参数。结果表明均匀链在有限尺寸下存在固有噪声劣势,边界响应分析指出需要扩展到二阶邻近耦合才能达到特定性能目标,为实际量子放大器系统的优化设计提供了定量指导。

Abstract: Directional quantum amplifiers enable weak-signal readout while protecting the source, but reservoir noise limits sensitivity. Finite-band suppression must reconcile frequency-dependent responses with fixed local couplings and finite rates. We construct adjacent-site loss channels attaining the known single-frequency minimum under local compatibility conditions, as an uncapped benchmark. For specified uniform and dimerized chains, we compare fixed reservoirs at matched drift, ports, high-gain band and complete gain/loss rate caps. At the representative cap $R=8$ and halfwidth $Ω=0.05$, nearest-neighbor arithmetic band average noise exceeds the unrestricted optimum by more than $1.01265$ quanta in the uniform chain for every finite $N\ge64$. Boundary-response bounds give necessary rates and targets requiring range two. At $N=64$, finite-auxiliary calculations retain a noise advantage under matched total coupling budgets; response bounds quantify noise-resolution tolerances. These theoretical results guide reservoir design and noise calibration for a proposed cryogenic microwave array with a $100$ kHz design band.

</details>


### [2] [Mechanical trisqueezing in circuit quantum acoustodynamics with SNAIL devices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20670)
*Yi-Zhou Bu,Matteo Fadel,Kai Xu,Heng Fan*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本文提出了一种电路量子声动力学(cQAD)架构,通过将机械振荡器与超导非线性非对称电感元件(SNAIL)耦合,实现三波混频相互作用以产生三压缩机械态,为机械量子系统中的非高斯资源生成提供了硬件高效路径。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有的电路量子声动力学实验虽然已实现双线性和四次(四波混频)操作,但由于器件固有的克尔非线性,无法获得如三压缩这样的奇数阶有效哈密顿量。而连续变量量子信息处理需要包含真正非高斯演化的通用门集,因此需要开发新的架构来实现奇数阶非线性相互作用,特别是三波混频,以生成非高斯量子态。

Method: 提出了一种cQAD架构:将高泛音体声波谐振器(HBAR)中的机械模式与超导非线性非对称电感元件(SNAIL)耦合。通过结合SNAIL的三次非线性和参数化磁通驱动,设计三波混频相互作用。从完整电路模型推导出有效的三压缩哈密顿量,确定驱动频率和磁通调制参数以从竞争项中分离出所需的相互作用,并量化当前制造能力下可实现的耦合强度。

Result: 该方案能够产生三压缩机械态,生成具有负值区域的特征性三角形星状维格纳函数,这是非高斯性的标志。理论分析表明,在与当前制造能力兼容的参数范围内,可以实现足够的耦合强度来生成这些非高斯量子态。

Conclusion: 该研究为机械量子系统中的非高斯资源生成建立了硬件高效的实现路径,扩展了电路量子声动力学的工具箱,为实现通用连续变量量子计算和增强量子极限传感奠定了基础。该架构克服了传统方法的局限性,使得在机械振荡器中实现奇数阶非线性相互作用成为可能。

Abstract: Continuous-variable quantum information processing with mechanical oscillators requires a uni- versal gate set, including a genuine non-Gaussian evolution. While circuit quantum acoustodynamics (cQAD) experiments have realized bilinear and quartic (four-wave mixing) operations by coupling mechanical modes to transmon qubits, the intrinsic Kerr nonlinearity of these devices precludes access to odd-order effective Hamiltonians such as trisqueezing. Here we propose a cQAD architec- ture in which a mechanical mode, hosted in a high-overtone bulk acoustic-wave resonator (HBAR), is coupled to a superconducting nonlinear asymmetric inductive element (SNAIL). By combining the cubic nonlinearity of the SNAIL with parametric flux driving, we engineer a three-wave mixing interaction that generates trisqueezed mechanical states, producing the characteristic triangular, star-shaped Wigner function with negative regions indicative of non-Gaussianity. We derive the effective trisqueezing Hamiltonian from the full circuit model, identify the drive frequencies and flux modulation required to isolate the desired interaction from competing terms, and quantify the achievable coupling strengths for parameters compatible with current fabrication capabilities. Our results establish a hardware-efficient route to non-Gaussian resource generation in mechanical quantum systems, extending the toolbox of cQAD toward universal continuous-variable quantum computation and enhanced quantum-limited sensing.

</details>


### [3] [Quantum Partial Information Decompositions, an Operational Order on Ensembles, and Direct Quantifications of Objective Information](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20694)
*Alexander Nico-Katz*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本文将经典的部分信息分解(PID)框架扩展到多体量子系统,构建了两种基于Holevo量的量子PID(QPID),并通过量子系综的可转换性建立了操作性偏序关系,最后将其应用于客观性场景中的扩展图态分析。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 经典的部分信息分解框架能够系统分析多体系统中的信息结构,但现有的量子扩展仅限于二体系统。为了处理更复杂的多体量子系统并量化其中的信息分布特性(如量子客观性问题),需要发展通用的多体量子PID框架及其构造方法。

Method: 1) 提出多体量子PID框架的简单扩展方案,突破现有二体限制;2) 基于经典最小互信息构建朴素的Holevo量子PID;3) 通过发展量子系综间的可转换性概念,建立系综上的操作性偏序关系;4) 利用该偏序关系构建更精细的Holevo量子PID;5) 将所构建的QPID应用于扩展图态中的客观性场景分析,直接量化客观信息。

Result: 成功构建了两种多体Holevo量子PID:一种基于经典最小互信息的朴素版本,另一种基于量子系综操作性偏序的精细版本。在扩展图态的客观性场景中,这些QPID能够直接量化客观信息,验证了方法的有效性和实用性。

Conclusion: 本研究为系统构造通用多体量子PID奠定了基础,引入了量子系综上具有操作意义的偏序关系,确立了量子PID作为独立研究对象的价值,并展示了其在量子信息问题(特别是量子客观性分析)中的直接适用性,为量子信息结构分析提供了新工具。

Abstract: The partial information decomposition (PID) framework is an extension of classical information theory that enables a systematic analysis of the structure of information in multipartite systems. We advance a simple extension of this framework enabling the construction of multipartite quantum PIDs (QPIDs); generalizing recent, exclusively bipartite, extensions. We construct two QPIDs of the Holevo quantity. First a naive QPID based on the classical minimum mutual information. Second a sophisticated QPID enabled by our development of an operational partial order on quantum ensembles in terms of ensemble (inter-)convertability. Finally, as a concrete example of how QPIDs can be applied, we investigate objectivity scenarios; using our Holevo QPIDs to directly quantify objective information in extended graph states. These findings ultimately establish a foundation upon which generic multipartite QPIDs can be systematically constructed, introduce an operational partial order on ensembles, advance QPIDs as interesting objects of study in their own right, and demonstrate their immediate applicability to problems in quantum information.

</details>


### [4] [Surviving readout errors and incomplete postselection in monitored quantum circuits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20712)
*Murtaza Ali Mir,Teemu Ojanen,Ali G. Moghaddam*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本文研究了读出误差对监测量子电路动力学的影响,发现在U(1)对称电路中,电荷锐化相变在有限误差率下仍然存在,并通过将涨落分解为量子和经典贡献,提出了一种最小缓解框架来从实验数据中直接定位相变。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 中途测量的读出误差比标准的电路末端测量更难缓解,因为误差会破坏后选择过程本身。理解读出误差如何影响监测量子电路中的测量诱导相变,对于在实际噪声环境中检测和表征这些相变至关重要,这在实验上具有重要意义。

Method: 针对具有U(1)对称性的监测量子电路,研究了读出误差对电荷锐化相变的影响。通过不完全后选择方法,将轨迹条件设定在不完美的记录而非真实记录上。关键技术是将电荷涨落分解为量子贡献和经典贡献,以此分析不完全后选择如何保留足够的轨迹记忆来抑制噪声诱导的混合效应。

Result: 研究发现电荷锐化相变在有限误差率下仍然存在,但锐化相的涨落消失被有限的稳态平台所取代。电荷涨落作为测量率的函数,在理想相变点附近呈现浅最小值,这反映了测量诱导纯化与噪声诱导混合之间的竞争。该最小值标志着不完全后选择仍保留足够轨迹记忆以抑制噪声混合的位置,即使在噪声环境中也能检测到相变的特征。

Conclusion: 电荷锐化相变以定量且实验可及的方式在读出误差下得以保持。通过将涨落分解为量子和经典贡献,研究提供了一个最小缓解框架,能够直接从实验数据中定位相变点。这为在实际噪声量子系统中研究测量诱导相变提供了切实可行的途径,对未来的实验实现具有重要指导意义。

Abstract: We study how readout errors affect dynamics in monitored quantum circuits. Such errors make postselection effectively incomplete, conditioning trajectories on imperfect rather than true records. Focusing on U(1)-symmetric circuits, we find that the charge-sharpening transition survives at a finite error rate, with the sharp phase's vanishing fluctuations replaced by a finite steady-state plateau. The charge fluctuations show a shallow minimum near the ideal transition point as a function of measurement rate, signaling the competition between measurement-induced purification and noise-induced mixing. By decomposing the fluctuations into quantum and classical contributions, we show the minimum marks where incomplete postselection still retains enough trajectory memory to attenuate noise-induced mixing, leaving a detectable signature of the transition even in a noisy setting. Mid-circuit measurements are far harder to mitigate against readout errors than standard end-of-circuit ones, because the errors corrupt the postselection itself; the decomposition therefore offers a minimal mitigation framework and a route to locate the transition directly from experimental data. Our results show that charge sharpening survives readout errors in a quantitative and experimentally accessible way.

</details>


### [5] [Quantum prepare-and-measure scenarios: concepts and methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20725)
*A. de Oliveira Junior,Santiago Zamora,Jonatan Bohr Brask,Rafael Chaves,Armin Tavakoli*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本文是一篇关于量子通信中"制备-测量"场景的教程性综述，系统介绍了该场景的基本框架、半设备无关方法、凸优化技术及其在量子密码学和设备认证中的应用。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 尽管制备-测量场景结构简单（发送方制备量子态，接收方进行测量），但它为探索经典与量子通信的差异、展示量子优势以及开发量子密码协议和设备认证提供了丰富的研究框架。现有文献缺乏系统的入门教程，研究新手需要全面的概念和技术基础来理解该领域并应用相关方法。

Method: 本文采用自下而上的教学方法，包括：1) 介绍制备-测量场景的基本框架和不同的通信约束表征方法；2) 重点阐述半设备无关的量子信息处理方法；3) 讲解用于表征该场景中可实现关联的现代凸优化技术；4) 通过实例演示这些工具的实际应用；5) 讨论未解决问题和未来研究方向。

Result: 本文为研究新手提供了完整的概念和技术基础，使其能够理解当前文献并在自己的研究中应用制备-测量方法。文章系统呈现了该领域的理论框架、分析工具和实践应用案例，并指出了开放性问题和未来研究方向。

Conclusion: 制备-测量场景是量子通信中最简单但极具研究价值的设置，通过半设备无关方法和凸优化技术可以有效表征其量子关联特性。本教程为该领域提供了系统的入门指导，有助于推动量子密码学、设备认证等应用的发展，并为未来研究奠定了基础。

Abstract: Prepare-and-measure scenarios are among the simplest and most widely used settings for quantum communication. A sender prepares a physical system in one of several possible states, which a receiver then measures. Despite this simple structure, these scenarios offer a rich framework for exploring the differences between classical and quantum communication, revealing quantum advantages, and developing protocols for quantum cryptography and device certification. In this tutorial, we give a ground-up introduction to prepare-and-measure scenarios and the methods used to study them. We introduce different frameworks for constraining and characterising communication, with an emphasis on semi-device-independent approaches to quantum information. We also cover modern convex-optimisation techniques for characterising the correlations achievable in these scenarios and illustrate how these tools can be applied in practice. Our aim is to give newcomers the conceptual and technical foundations needed to approach the current literature and apply prepare-and-measure methods in their own research. We conclude with a discussion of open problems and directions for future work.

</details>


### [6] [Alternating Squeezing and Photon Subtraction for Optical Cat-State Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20728)
*Johanna Conrad,Fabian Steinlechner,René Sondenheimer*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本文研究了通过交替压缩和光子数减除操作生成大振幅、高保真度薛定谔猫态的方案，提出了保真度约束下的多分支联合优化方法，在有限的压缩资源和光子数分辨能力约束下，相比传统单次光子减除方法实现了保真度和成功概率的提升。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 光学领域中薛定谔猫态是量子信息处理和量子增强测量的重要非高斯资源。虽然从压缩真空态中减除光子已被证明可用于生成此类态，但在保持高保真度和高预示概率的同时扩展到大振幅仍然具有挑战性。现有方法通常只针对单一预示结果进行优化，未能充分利用实验资源，且在实际实验约束（如有限的压缩资源和光子数分辨能力）下性能受限。

Method: 本文将猫态生成问题重新表述为保真度约束下的多分支优化问题，联合优化所有满足规定保真度阈值的光子数结果在同一实验设置下的总概率。研究了交替压缩和光子数减除操作的方案，推导了条件态、猫态保真度和预示概率的解析表达式。在考虑实验约束（可用压缩资源和光子数分辨能力限制）的情况下，系统分析了不同架构配置，包括单次减除端点配置和真正的交替分支配置，并评估了资源权衡、对实验参数偏差的敏感性以及预示损耗的影响。

Result: 对于普通光子减除方案，多分支优化方法提供了更具操作性的可用生成概率度量，并可能产生不同于单分支最优的参数配置。交替压缩与光子数减除方案在不必增加压缩资源的情况下，相比普通光子减除实现了保真度和成功概率的改进。在不限制光子数分辨率时，最优交替架构退化为两种单次减除端点配置之一，具体取决于可用的压缩资源。当光子数分辨率受到额外限制时，真正的交替分支配置会变得更具优势。解析表达式使得高效的多分支优化和系统性资源分析成为可能。

Conclusion: 本文建立了一套完整的保真度约束多分支优化框架，用于在实验资源约束下生成高质量薛定谔猫态。研究结果表明，多分支联合优化策略能够更有效地利用实验资源，交替压缩与光子数减除方案在特定资源预算下优于传统方法。通过推导的解析表达式和系统性分析，该工作为根据给定资源预算选择最佳架构提供了指导，有助于推进量子信息处理和量子测量中大振幅猫态的实际应用。

Abstract: Schrödinger cat states are important non-Gaussian resources for quantum information processing and quantum-enhanced metrology. Although photon subtraction from squeezed vacuum states has proven useful for generating such states in the optical domain, scaling to large amplitudes while maintaining high fidelity and heralding probability remains challenging. To address these obstacles, we analyze alternating squeezing and photon-number-subtraction operations. Rather than optimizing for a single heralding outcome, we formulate cat-state generation as a fidelity-constrained multibranch problem, jointly optimizing the total probability of all photon-number outcomes satisfying a prescribed fidelity threshold at one common experimental setting. Already for plain photon subtraction, this provides a more operational measure of the usable generation probability and can favor parameter configurations that differ from single-branch optima. Considering experimental constraints on available squeezing and photon-number-resolving capability, we demonstrate improvements in fidelity and success probability compared with plain photon subtraction, without necessarily increasing the required squeezing resources. Without restrictions on photon-number resolution, we find that the optimal architecture of the alternating scheme reduces to one of two single-subtraction endpoint configurations, depending on the available squeezing resources. When photon-number resolution is additionally limited, genuinely alternating branches can become advantageous. We derive analytical expressions for the conditional states, cat-state fidelities, and heralding probabilities, enabling efficient multibranch optimization and a systematic analysis of resource trade-offs, sensitivity to experimental parameter deviations, and heralding loss. Together, these results identify the architecture best suited to a given resource budget.

</details>

TL;DR: 本文通过构建加权平方和层次结构中每一层的完整二体见证族,系统性地解决了费米子量子边缘问题,并提供了验证候选二粒子约化密度矩阵物理性的统一构造框架。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 费米子量子边缘问题的核心挑战在于判定候选二粒子约化密度矩阵是否来自物理可实现的多体波函数。传统方法依赖寻找个别可表示性条件,缺乏系统性和完备性。本文旨在建立一个完备的、可构造的代数框架来彻底刻画所有二体见证,从而统一解决该问题。

Abstract: Quantum imaginary-time evolution (QITE) provides a systematic route to ground-state preparation, but its cost grows rapidly with the locality $D$ of the unitary approximation, for which the local Pauli basis scales as $4^D$. We introduce Sparse-QITE, a regularized formulation that promotes compact generators, reducing the number of quantum operations and yielding circuits with fewer gates and lower depth. We derive rigorous bounds on the sparsification error and benchmark the method in one-dimensional transverse-field Ising and antiferromagnetic Heisenberg models. Sparse-QITE exhibits a controllable sparsity--accuracy trade-off while retaining strong compression even in the high-accuracy regime. In scalability experiments with respect to $D$, the active support grows much more slowly than in standard QITE and, in several regimes, approximately saturates over the domain sizes studied. After circuit construction and transpilation, some of the largest-domain cases require only about $1\%$ of the standard-QITE gate count while maintaining high accuracy. We further introduce adaptive and active-discovery variants: the former selects the regularization strength dynamically during the evolution, while the latter reduces the number of Pauli expectation values that need to be evaluated and, consequently, the measurement requirements. These results establish sparsity-promoting regularization as a route toward more resource-efficient QITE. Abstract: Collisions between atoms and molecules govern how energy is exchanged and redistributed in the gas phase, underpinning phenomena from spectral line broadening and interstellar molecular excitation to the cooling and trapping of molecules. A quantitative account of these collisions requires connecting the theoretical description of the interaction between the collision partners to measurable quantities such as cross sections. However, scattering theory as presented in quantum mechanics textbooks does not readily translate into numerical methods. Here, we formulate the scattering problem in Dirac notation, as customary in present-day quantum mechanics courses, and show how to build a practical numerical framework from it. We derive the "coupled channel" representation of the scattering problem with correct asymptotic behaviour and introduce Numerov's method to obtain time-independent wavefunctions, from which we calculate cross sections, the observables measured in scattering experiments. Starting from single channel scattering, we build up to the realistic collision of a ground-state atom with a diatomic molecule.

Combining this bound with block acceptance gives an entanglement-fidelity yield bound, which we maximize over evaluated wavelengths subject to fidelity and classical link-budget requirements. For the Steane [[7,1,3]] code at 5 km per traversal and -10 dBm per classical channel, the largest fidelity certificate occurs near 1537 nm, whereas the largest yield bound occurs near 1551 nm. Along the 1.25 Gb/s classical link budget with 0.2% base error, the optimized yield bound falls below 10^-6 near 10 km per traversal, while fidelity remains certified at 0.99 or higher at longer spans. These results distinguish fidelity and yield constraints and identify certified operating regions for quantum-augmented networks sharing optical fiber.

Abstract: Determining whether quantum states can be interconverted by a prescribed set of transformations is a fundamental problem in quantum information. Here we develop a general classification framework based on conserved operators (COs), i.e., not necessarily hermitian matrices having the state of interest as an eigenstate. We first show that COs belonging to the algebra generating the allowed transformations can be obtained from the kernel of a quantum covariance matrix. Their number determines the dimension of the accessible orbit, while their algebraic structure and eigenvalues provide further constraints on state convertibility. We establish general bounds on the number of COs and prove that highest-weight states, like separable states in the case of $n$ qudits, attain the maximum. We also show how these operators generate parent Hamiltonians having the characterized state as an exact ground state. We then also consider positive zero-eigenvalue COs, which encode forbidden directions of reduced density matrices and incorporate rank-based constraints into the same framework. Applying this approach to multiqubit SLOCC, we obtain complementary classifications from algebraic and rank COs. While for $n\leq 3$ qubits both lead to the usual classes, for $n=4$ qubits they distinguish 17 and 20 types separately, and 29 when combined. The combined classification yields 287 fixed types and $4$ continuous families for $n=5$ qubits, and 4926 fixed types and 147 continuous families for $n=6$ qubits, providing, to our knowledge, the most refined exhaustive classification of SLOCC-convertibility classes based on such data.

Conclusion: 通过引入反对易索引和利用局域性特性，monoprop实现了反直觉的性能突破——固定算符项数时增加系统规模反而加速计算。该框架具有优异的并行扩展性和内存效率，为大规模量子化学模拟和自适应量子电路优化提供了强大工具，开源发布将推动量子算法研究的进展。

Abstract: We introduce monoprop, an open-source, massively parallel operator-propagation framework for fermion and qubit bases. For $N$ qubits or modes, existing codes represent each of the $K$ tracked operator terms (Pauli strings or products of Majorana operators) as a $2N$-bit string, testing each for anticommutation with the gate at $O(KN)$ cost per gate. Here, we introduce the anticommutation index which rapidly looks up the terms anticommuting with a gate. A local gate changes only the terms that touch its sites, and when terms are truncated to act on few sites, only a fraction $\sim1/N$ touch any given qubit or mode, and we use this to prove the time per gate scales as $O(K/N)$. This counterintuitive result tells us that for a fixed number of terms, increasing the number of sites can make things faster. We demonstrate this numerically, and show monoprop scales almost ideally to 8192 cores, tracking $9.5\times10^{10}$ terms. Beyond propagation, we present memory-efficient surrogate graphs, which record one propagation and replay it at new angles, for fast energy re-estimation and parameter gradients, and an efficient method for finding energy-minimising gates and optimal angles for simulating adaptive quantum-chemistry circuits.

</details>

Abstract: Drone-delivery planning combines routing decisions with operational constraints such as payload capacity and limited onboard energy, resulting in combinatorial optimization problems that can become costly to encode directly in binary form. Payload- and energy-infeasible missions are removed during preprocessing, while the reduced QUBO is solved using exact enumeration, simulated annealing (SA), and the Quantum Approximate Optimization Algorithm (QAOA), with mixed-integer linear programming (MILP) used as a classical reference. Combined payload-and-energy preprocessing reduces the mean number of QUBO variables by $29.60\%$ and quadratic interactions by $49.20\%$, while reducing mean SA runtime by $31.26\%$. In contrast, the evaluated shallow statevector-based QAOA configuration achieves lower feasibility and is restricted to small QUBO instances. These results indicate that, in the considered setting, the main practical benefit of the hybrid architecture comes from classical feasibility preprocessing that reduces the binary search space before QUBO-based optimization.

Abstract: Tests of gravity-mediated entanglement aim to determine whether two massive objects in spatial superposition can become entangled through their mutual gravitational interaction. We find finite parameter windows where the same state is Bell-nonlocal and teleportation-useful, but these windows shrink as the wave packets become broader or the dephasing becomes stronger. We also show that a residual electrostatic branch interaction can coherently cancel the gravitational nonlocal phase and strongly suppress the operational signal. The results identify finite-width and electrostatic tolerance boundaries that should be considered in future tests of gravity-mediated entanglement.

Abstract: Recent studies have reported promising results from quantum machine learning in drug discovery, healthcare, materials science and cybersecurity. Research includes comparisons with classical machine learning algorithms and the development of sequential hybrid pipelines. We propose that fusion can improve prediction when classical and quantum representations capture complementary predictive relationships. Our classical-quantum multi-view framework preserves the full classical input alongside simulated quantum features. Concatenation and attention combine the views; single-view comparisons, replacement and masking controls assess both views' contributions. Controlled angular-polynomial experiments distinguish targets adequately represented by a single view from those that depend on complementary angular and polynomial relationships. For these mixed targets, fusion improves prediction by combining the relevant feature sets, while matched classical representations recover the same benefit, linking the improvement to their complementary predictive content. Controlled classification tests further show that gains depend on alignment between the chosen representation and the target, and on a suitable fusion rule. Benchmark validation shows improved mean predictive performance over classical baselines on Wine, Wisconsin Diagnostic Breast Cancer, three-class Fashion-MNIST and Steel Plates Faults within the evaluated protocols. Classical replacement and masking controls remain competitive, classical baselines lead on Digits and Covertype, and outcomes with missing or corrupted views depend on the available inputs. These findings support evaluating quantum representations for their complementary predictive contribution and matching the representation and fusion strategy to the target relationships.

Abstract: We give a proof of the cubic parallel-repetition bound for finite two-player entangled games using quantum conditional relative entropy. For a game with entangled value $1-\varepsilon<1$ and answer alphabets $\mathcal A,\mathcal B$, its $n$-fold repeated value is at most \[

Abstract: Long-range, genuine multipartite entanglement (GME) between distant parties in a quantum network is a crucial resource for distributed quantum information processing. Through controlled manipulation of short-range GME states in the network via stochastic local operations aided by classical communication (SLOCC), long-range GME can be established between selected target nodes on demand. Optimizing such multipartite entanglement distribution protocols under constraints imposed by the network architecture, target-node set, and available resource budget is a key challenge in resource-efficient manipulation of GME. Abstract: We investigate the Casimir force on thin suspended dielectric layers covered with graphene, both theoretically and experimentally. Thermal effects of the Casimir force on graphene occur at separations significantly smaller compared to ordinary metals and dielectrics. At such small separations, the Casimir force is sufficiently strong to be measured experimentally. However, the presence of substrates to support the graphene significantly reduces the enhancement. We show theoretically that replacing the solid substrate beneath graphene with a thin dielectric membrane partially restores this strong thermal dependence. One of the interacting objects is a solid gold plate. The other object is a suspended dielectric membrane made of either 15 nm of silicon oxide or 13 nm silicon nitride, covered with graphene. At a separation of $\sim$500 nm, theoretical predictions yield changes in the Casimir pressure of up to 54 % and 40 % for silicon oxide and silicon nitride, respectively, when the temperature is increased from 10 K to 294 K. Measurements were performed for graphene on a suspended silicon nitride membrane at room temperature, using a gold-coated sphere attached to a cantilever as the force sensor. The measured force gradient is consistent with the theoretical prediction, although the thermal dependence could not be resolved, in part because of uncertainties in the optical properties of silicon nitride. Replacing a bulk substrate with a thin dielectric membrane covered with graphene provides a substantial reduction of the Casimir force, which may facilitate the stable operation of neutral nanomechanical elements at small separations.

Although the model is exactly solvable, simple analytic expressions for important physical quantities are generally unavailable. We derive rigorous upper and lower bounds for the ground-state energy and boson occupation number across the quantum phase transition at finite frequency ratios. Previous work reduced this cost by restricting the trainable phases to predefined sparse patterns, such as a diagonal or a grid, which however may not be optimal for a specific target state. In this work, we address this limitation by introducing a method that learns which phases are needed together with their values, using a differentiable mask. A sparsity penalty encourages the removal of unnecessary phases, while temperature annealing gradually turns the masks into binary decisions. We compare the proposed method with fully parameterized and predefined sparse ansatzes on Fourier and Haar-random target states. The evaluation considers both the minimum number of phases required to reach different infidelity thresholds and the overall trade-off between fidelity and phases required, measured through Pareto frontiers and hypervolume. The results show that learning the location of the phases together with their values provides competitive or improved fidelity--resource trade-offs, although the advantage depends on the target state and qudit dimension.

Abstract: Robust quantum control usually assumes one of two extremes: the error is of a known form and only its strength is unknown, or nothing at all is known about it. In practice one often sits in between. The size of a static error is unknown, but its class is known, for example, it only shifts energy levels, or it acts on each subsystem separately. We ask how fast a control can cancel, to first order, every error in such a class. A time-independent Hamiltonian can do this if and only if all errors in the class have zero diagonal in a common basis. In that case an equally spaced energy ladder in this basis does it after one period. A lower bound that holds for any time-dependent control with bounded drive power shows that this simple constant control is optimal for several natural classes, including unknown level shifts and unknown local errors on two subsystems. Completely unknown errors need a time-dependent control, and there, knowing the error class shortens the minimum time by a factor $\sqrt{d^2+1}$. So partial knowledge of the error changes not only how long an optimal robust control takes, but also its form.

Abstract: Reliable characterization of quantum states and quantum processes is central to the development of scalable quantum computing. Standard characterization techniques such as full quantum state tomography become rapidly impractical as system size increases. In this work we present two software-oriented fidelity estimation protocols that avoid full tomographic reconstruction while remaining experimentally relevant for noisy intermediate-scale quantum devices. The first protocol is direct fidelity estimation (DFE), where the fidelity between a target state and an experimentally prepared state is estimated from importance-sampled Pauli measurements. The second protocol is a survival-probability-based method that reconstructs fidelity from experimentally accessible moments of a noisy evolution and its approximate inverse, and is intrinsically robust against state-preparation-and-measurement (SPAM) errors. We discuss the theoretical basis of both approaches, analyze their noise sensitivity, and present numerical evidence on representative multiqubit states under depolarizing, dephasing, and amplitude-damping channels. For DFE we show that attenuation-aware correction improves robustness against measurement noise. For the survival-probability approach we show that an $L_2$-optimized expansion substantially outperforms naive Taylor truncation in high-noise regimes.

Abstract: Finite rate randomness expansion (RE) which is secure against quantum adversaries is a crucial information processing task which is well studied in the case of two non-communicating parties sharing Bell non-local correlations in a device independent manner. In this paper we show that contextual correlations with only a single localized party are sufficient for RE in a semi-device independent (SDI) manner. We propose a template SDI-RE protocol which is applicable for arbitrary $N$-cycle non-contextuality (NC) inequalities and derive its RE rate under finite statistics. As a special case, we also highlight the advantages of the situation when RE is constrained by Clauser-Horne-Shimony-Holt (CHSH) inequality but with characterized measurements implemented by only a single party. Our template protocol also incorporates non-ideal statistics which may not satisfy perfect commutativity and perfect repeatability, both of which are necessary in a loophole-free test of NC inequalities. As such, several recent experimental implementations of significantly loophole-free tests of NC inequalities can be modified for secure RE following our approach.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Method: 1. 理论分析:证明串行电路由于其状态位于布洛赫球面上,存在"相位锁定"现象,即所有参数在每个输入处仅沿一个复方向移动状态,导致切向方向最多为2E+2个(E为编码预算),仅比所需的2E+1个傅里叶系数多一个;而具有相同编码预算的双量子比特对照电路通常至少有6E+6个切向方向。2. 实验验证:在固定步数预算内,对比串行电路和对照电路在100次运行中达到最优解的次数,测试场景包括平谱目标的精确损失函数和三个真实世界序列数据集,使用梯度下降、Adam和Levenberg-Marquardt三种优化器。

Result: 在E=32的平谱目标测试中,串行电路在梯度下降、Adam和Levenberg-Marquardt下分别仅有3、53和83次运行收敛,而对照电路达到99、100和100次。在三个真实世界序列数据集上,串行电路在Adam下最多9次运行达到最小二乘最优,在Levenberg-Marquardt下为32至93次,对照电路分别至少98次和全部100次收敛。串行电路在Adam下的留出误差最高约为对照电路的1.5倍,但在Levenberg-Marquardt的典型运行中与对照电路相当。

Conclusion: 单量子比特重上传串行电路虽然理论上具有与多量子比特电路相同的函数表达能力,但由于相位锁定导致的切向方向严重受限,在一阶训练中收敛性能显著劣于多量子比特对照电路。这种用宽度换深度的策略在实际训练中并非"免费",串行电路的收敛可靠性明显更低,尤其在编码预算增大时差距扩大,表明电路架构选择对训练效率有重要影响。

Abstract: Angle-encoded variational quantum circuits implement truncated Fourier series. For one-dimensional inputs, a single-qubit re-uploading (serial) circuit represents every function that a multi-qubit circuit with the same encoding budget $E$ can; we find that this exchange of width for depth is not free in first-order training. Because its state lives on the Bloch sphere, all parameters of the serial circuit move the state, at each input, along one complex direction, which we call \emph{phase-locking}. We prove that this costs no Fourier direction but leaves no direction to spare: however many parameters it has, the serial circuit has at most $2E+2$ tangent directions, one more than its $2E+1$ Fourier coefficients need, while a two-qubit control with the same encoding budget and model class generically has at least $6E+6$. The cost in training is measured against this control, as the number of runs out of 100 that reach the optimum within a fixed step budget. On an exact loss with flat-spectrum targets the gap grows with $E$; at $E=32$ the serial circuit converges in 3, 53 and 83 runs under gradient descent, Adam and Levenberg--Marquardt, the control in 99, 100 and 100. Result: 四探测器方案能够达到量子Fisher信息所给出的理论最大精度,且无需对系统损耗进行表征。系统误差会在重复测量之间引入关联,限制了通过增加重复次数提升精度的效果。在考虑这些关联后,四探测器方案在时间延迟估计精度上显著优于双探测器方案。实验结果显示,在已执行的重复测量中,四探测器方案使色散改善了2倍,当两种方案接近各自的系统误差下限时,改善因子可达4倍。

Abstract: In this work we study the application of two-photon correlated quantum states for high-precision time estimation. To achieve this, photon pairs were fed into a Hong-Ou-Mandel polarization interferometer to generate N00N entangled states with $N = 2$. Two- and four-detector schemes were considered, and the achievable time estimation precision of each was studied, with particular emphasis on the system's Fisher information in each case. Our results reveal that using a four-detector scheme, enabling the measurement of both coincidence and photon-bunching terms, achieves the maximum precision given by the quantum Fisher information, without the need of characterizing system losses. We also show that correlations between repetitions arise due to systematic errors, limiting the precision enhancement that can be obtained by increasing the number of repetitions. Taking these correlations into account, we show that the four-detector scheme outperforms the two-detector scheme in the precision of time-delay estimation. With the experimentally determined calibration uncertainties, the four-detector scheme improves the dispersion by a factor of 2 for the repetitions performed, reaching up to a factor of 4 as both schemes approach their systematic floors.

Abstract: We examine how a finite many-body system can function as an effective charger for a single-qubit quantum battery, using the Lipkin-Meshkov-Glick model as a paradigmatic setting. As our figure of merit, we consider the battery ergotropy while explicitly resolving the energetic flows across the composite system. In the absence of intra-charger interactions, we show that the generation of non-zero ergotropy is controlled by the competition between collective coupling and spectral mismatch. We further demonstrate that even passive positive-temperature charger states can generate finite battery ergotropy, provided the charger is sufficiently large and sufficiently hot. Intra-charger interactions restructure the charger spectrum and qualitatively modify the available energetic pathways. While they can enhance the amount of energy accessible for charging, they can simultaneously divert energy into internal many-body and battery-charger interaction channels, producing an energetic impedance to direct charger-to-battery transfer. Consequently, battery ergotropy alone is insufficient to characterize an effective many-body charger: the useful work generated and its energetic provenance must be assessed together.

Abstract: Classical simulation of quantum many-body dynamics is challenged by the rapid growth of operator complexity, particularly in higher dimensions where tensor-network methods become increasingly demanding. We develop state-adapted Pauli-weight propagation based on generalized $m$-body mean-field projections, which compress many-body observables into optimal low-body representations in a covariance geometry induced by the initial state. Combined with adaptive Krylov-like restricted Heisenberg dynamics, this provides an alternative to conventional Hilbert--Schmidt Pauli-weight truncation. We derive necessary and conditional sufficient bounds on low-body approximation accuracy using standard operator complexity measures and characterize possible dynamical failure mechanisms of the state-adapted method. Numerical benchmarks demonstrate substantial state-dependent accuracy gains, while identifying regimes of reduced accuracy or violations of Pauli spectral bounds. For a three-dimensional system of $80$ interacting spins, our method achieves strong body-order convergence, close agreement with independently converging tensor-network simulations, and substantially smaller sparse Pauli representations. These results establish state-adapted Pauli propagation as a promising approach to classical simulation of higher-dimensional coherent many-body dynamics.

Exchange conserves total spin, so the reachable readout distributions are fixed by the total-spin content of the initial product state. For the two-spin singlet-triplet encoding we derive a selection rule -- outcomes with k triplet pairs are bounded by the initial weight in sectors S <= k (S = 0 excluded for k = 1) -- and prove by a transportation argument that it is also sufficient: the reachable distributions form the polytope cut out by the rule, and its total-variation floor is attained. Products of singlets and T0's forbid whole outcome classes; the Neel state forbids none; the three-spin EO code has no floor. At six logical qubits the three-spin chain reaches KL = 0.011 nat at depth N and 0.004 at 2N, below the bond-dimension-4 MPS; the two-spin chain from the Neel state reaches 0.014 at 2N and 0.004 at 3N, against 0.28-1.5 from singlet/triplet products for the first teacher. Final states lie largely outside the code space: leakage is a resource, not an error. No arrangement of disjoint pulses meets every pair in fewer than N-1 rounds, and at depth 2N the chain distils as well as a 2D array or non-local couplings. Sector-restricted simulation extends this to 10-14 logical qubits (up to 30 dots).

Abstract: We introduce patchwork, a lattice-surgery compiler for arbitrary multi-qubit Pauli measurements on a prescribed narrow-corridor two-dimensional surface-code layout. Abstract: Greenberger-Horne-Zeilinger (GHZ) states are multipartite entangled quantum states that play a central role in quantum information theory, with applications in quantum communication, cryptography, and distributed quantum computing. A major experimental goal is to create high-dimensional GHZ states using multiple entangled parties. Krenn, Gu and Zeilinger discovered a surprising bridge between these quantum-optical experiments and graph theory: certain photonic experiments can be represented by edge-coloured, edge-weighted graphs, and the dimension of the produced GHZ state equals the number of feasible monochromatic inherited vertex colourings. This led Krenn and Gu to conjecture that every GHZ graph with more than four vertices has dimension at most $2$. A counterexample would give a new high-dimensional multipartite GHZ construction, while a proof would clarify fundamental resource limitations in quantum optics. It is known that the conjecture holds for graphs of vertex connectivity at most $2$ [MFCS 2024], and that simple GHZ graphs with $n>4$ vertices have dimension less than $n/\sqrt{2}$ [Quantum 2024]. However, no general upper bound depending only on vertex connectivity was known. We prove that every simple connected GHZ graph $G$ with at least four vertices has dimension at most $2^{κ(G)-1}+\binom{κ(G)}{2}$, where $κ(G)$ is the vertex connectivity of $G$. We also prove new bounds in terms of edge connectivity and maximum degree.

We develop a detailed energy budget, identifying cryogenic cooling as the dominant baseline contribution before quantum error correction (QEC). The comparison between the QFT and the classical FFT reveals that, for any quantum workload operating under a polynomially bounded sampling regime, the crossover in energy consumption occurs at smaller problem sizes than the crossover in execution time. Thus, quantum computing can become energetically competitive before achieving a speedup in execution time. We then incorporate surface-code QEC and analyze the energy costs of QEC cycles, gate synthesis, and decoding. At scale, instrumentation and decoding become dominant contributions, while cryogenic cooling imposes a constraint on scalability. Whether the long-time dynamics is dominated by scars is determined by the real parts of the eigenvalues, which are set by the ratio between the two relaxation rates $γ_{\pm}$ describing spin flips down-to-up or up-to-down respectively. For asymmetric dissipation ($γ_+ > γ_-$) the scarred eigenvalues have the smallest real parts: the resulting enhanced fidelity oscillations are further stabilized by staggered jump operators. Abstract: Fault-tolerant quantum computing (FTQC) introduces substantial systems-level complexity through quantum error correction (QEC), making debugging FTQC programs challenging. Existing quantum debugging approaches primarily target logical-level circuits and do not naturally support debugging within realistic FTQC execution flows. We present SynDebug, the first systems-level debugging framework for FTQC programs. SynDebug introduces lightweight assertion-based debugging primitives that operate directly within encoded FTQC infrastructures while avoiding additional logical surface area and minimizing temporal overhead. We evaluate SynDebug across multiple QEC code families, code distances, and encoded workloads, including state preparation, entanglement validation, and Shor-style programs. Our results demonstrate that SynDebug enables practical FTQC debugging with an average 1.8% additional circuit depth overhead (0% in most cases) and no measurable increase in the logical error rate, showing that systems-level debugging for large-scale FTQC applications is both feasible and practical.

Abstract: The classical capacity of a noisy optical channel is the maximum rate at which information can be transmitted reliably, given a constraint on the mean input energy. For the ubiquitous bosonic Gaussian channels, which include standard models of loss and noise in fibre and free-space optical links, determining this rate requires addressing whether entanglement between channel uses or non-Gaussian signals can outperform Gaussian encodings. More precisely, the question is whether the energy-constrained Holevo information is additive across arbitrarily many channel uses and coincides with its Gaussian counterpart. We answer these fundamental questions in the affirmative for every single-mode bosonic Gaussian channel at every input energy. Namely, we prove that independently prepared Gaussian states with Gaussian-distributed random displacements achieve the capacity, including in the low-energy regime where squeezing the signals competes with encoding information in their displacement. Our proof connects an entanglement inequality to a sharp, additive lower bound on output entropy. We then characterise communication above capacity: under a strict total photon-number cutoff, the probability of correct decoding decreases exponentially with the number of channel uses, and we derive explicit bounds on the decay exponent. When only the mean photon number is constrained, a simple energy-rescaling formula determines exactly the entire asymptotic rate--error trade-off: the capacity at mean photon number $N$ and allowed average decoding error $ε$ equals the vanishing-error capacity at mean photon number $N/(1-ε)$. Together, these results identify the ultimate limits of classical communication for some of the most fundamental models of optical communication.

Abstract: Classical simulation of magic state factories, including distillation, cultivation, and code switching, is challenging because of their substantial non-Clifford content. We introduce $\texttt{magna}$ (magic-state-factory assessment via global network algebra), a classical simulator that simulates such protocols as cheaply as Clifford circuits. $\texttt{magna}$ expresses the figures of merit of a protocol, namely the acceptance rate and the fidelity of the accepted states, as tensor networks, which it reduces globally by exploiting a structural correspondence with transversal gates on quantum codes. $\texttt{magna}$ further supports feedforward conditioned on arbitrary functions of mid-circuit measurement outcomes, including decoder outputs. We simulate Bravyi--Haah distillation with up to 100 output magic states at a cost that does not grow exponentially in the number of outputs, and achieve state-of-the-art sampling times for distance-5 magic state cultivation on both color codes and RP$^2$ surface codes. Finally, we validate nonlinear feedforward on a logical-level distillation protocol with 15 rounds of decoder-dependent feedforward. $\texttt{magna}$ aims to contribute to the key infrastructure for the large-scale evaluation and optimization of magic state factories.

Abstract: The quantum-trajectory description of a noisy quantum circuit is not unique: every Kraus representation of the noise channel defines a different unraveling, i.e., a different ensemble of pure states that averages to the same density matrix. While all unravelings share the same expectation values of observables, they generally differ in the amount of quantum resources carried by the individual trajectories. Here we study non-stabilizerness, or magic, quantified by the stabilizer Rényi entropy, in quantum-trajectory unravelings of Haar-random and $T$-doped random Clifford brickwork circuits subject to local dephasing noise of strength $p$. The canonical unraveling in terms of Pauli jumps is Clifford and leaves magic untouched, so that trajectories retain the Haar-random value of non-stabilizerness. Exploring the full two-parameter family of minimal unravelings, we find that the trajectory-averaged magic can be substantially reduced by a state-independent choice of Kraus operators, and that the optimal choice is not in general the projective-measurement unraveling, but a two-outcome generalized measurement in which the Kraus operators correspond to a weak measurement of the noisy qubit. The optimal unraveling reduces the extensive magic density of the trajectories by a $p$-dependent factor without altering its linear scaling with system size. At the same time, it produces a very broad distribution of magic over trajectories, with a sizable fraction of trajectories retaining Haar-random magic. Since the cost of stabilizer-based classical simulation grows exponentially with the magic of the state, these high-magic trajectories, rather than the average, control the simulation cost. Our results delineate the potential and the limitations of unraveling optimization as a route to the classical simulation of noisy quantum dynamics with stabilizer methods.

</details>

Abstract: Quantum low-density parity-check (qLDPC) codes have a canonical mixed state---the normalized projector onto the code space---that lies at the intersection of quantum error correction and quantum many-body physics, yet the stability of its information-theoretic structure under local noise remains poorly understood. Recent progress on mixed-state phases of matter has identified the spatial decay of conditional mutual information (CMI) $I(A:C|B)$ as a key diagnostic of such stability, analogous to the role of the spectral gap for quantum ground states. We prove that, for the normalized code-projector state of a $\mathbb{Z}_d$ qLDPC stabilizer code under local Pauli noise, the CMI across a buffer $B$ separating $A$ from $C$ decays exponentially with the width of $B$ in two complementary noise regimes independent of system size. At weak noise, this holds for full-system tripartitions, where correlations between the two sides require an error cluster to cross the buffer, an exponentially unlikely event. At strong noise, exponential decay holds whenever the buffer supports no logical operator, since correlations can persist only along rare chains of buffer qudits that remain informative. Under an additional space-uniformity assumption, we establish the robustness of Levin--Wen topological entropy. On finite-dimensional lattices of size $n$, the weak-noise bound further implies a constant-layer quasi-local Petz recovery channel of range $O(\poly(\log n)/\varepsilon)$ that recovers the noiseless state within trace-distance error $\varepsilon$. Both CMI bounds apply to general qLDPC interaction graphs, including geometries where such constant-depth quasi-local recovery need not exist. Thus, the exponential decay of the noise-induced CMI provides a general criterion for mixed-state phase stability.

Abstract: Significant efforts are currently being directed toward the implementation of large-scale quantum information processors, but realizing useful quantum advantage also requires the development of efficient quantum algorithms. While for many practical problems the dominant resource costs are associated with a small set of computational primitives, optimizing them in the quantum setting presents unique challenges, including a reversibility constraint absent from ordinary classical computation. Here we introduce an evolutionary framework in which large language models (LLMs) propose circuit designs and an independent evaluator verifies and scores them. We apply this framework to tasks spanning numerical computation, chemistry, optimization, quantum simulation, and number theory. In seven of the eight tasks, the discovered circuits significantly reduce both Toffoli and qubit counts relative to the state of the art. In the eighth, the search discovers a new reversible architecture for prime-field modular inversion. By handling reversibility differently, the architecture makes divsteps -- a classical algorithm previously impractical for reversible integer arithmetic -- viable and substantially improves the Toffoli-qubit trade-off for modular inversion. These results open the door for evaluator-guided LLM co-design of new algorithmic structures for quantum computation.

Abstract: The RNS formalism describing string theory on $\mathrm{AdS}_3 \times \mathbf{S}^3 \times \mathrm{K3}$ supported by pure NS-NS flux is in principle straightforward to write down. One can directly check that the worldsheet theory reproduces the correct chiral spectrum expected from supergravity by analyzing the BRST cohomology. However, when one attempts to construct the worldsheet partition function using the standard rules of the RNS formalism by appropriately summing over spin structures to ensure worldsheet modular invariance, one finds a result in tension with spacetime supersymmetry. We explain why this is the case and, more importantly, propose a resolution. Specifically, we fix the physical state projection for the partition function uniquely by requiring spacetime supersymmetry. Perhaps surprisingly, the projected worldsheet partition function does not factorize into a bosonic and a fermionic part and involves functions that are not modular covariant. As a consistency check, we show that our prescription for the worldsheet partition function reproduces the supergravity spectrum for both Type IIB and IIA theories. Furthermore, we compute the elliptic genus of the dual CFT from the worldsheet, which has both a holomorphic short string and a non-holomorphic long string contribution. The latter displays characteristics of a mock modular function, albeit with some key differences, while the former has the tantalizing structure of a symmetric orbifold. We also point out the worldsheet construction of a non-supersymmetric theory, whose spectrum is free of tachyons.

</details>

Abstract: Numerical conformal bootstrap bounds are commonly called rigorous, yet as published they are not theorems: they hold at sampled points rather than on regions of parameter space, for truncated rational approximations of the conformal blocks, for a finite list of spins, and up to floating-point rounding. The analytic inputs for the mixed $σ$-$ε$ system, which would remove the spin-2 gap assumption, are also proved. The same methods prevent common practical failure modes and can make bootstrap searches faster: region bounds turn the mapping of an island into a handful of one-dimensional bisections. Abstract: We construct an observer-centric S-matrix in the expanding Poincaré patch of four-dimensional de Sitter space. Coordinate-aligned configurations are recovered as special cases, and the exact massless limit and the compact-direction pressures are obtained. These results establish how the relative orientation between a Lorentz-violating background and an anisotropic compactification lattice enters the renormalized vacuum energy and its directional response.

We find that the transient maxima of antiflatness, capacity of entanglement, and nonlocal magic occur on the same characteristic timescale as the maximum rate of entanglement growth. We consider scattering of an arbitrary number of incoming and outgoing nonrelativistic objects interacting only through gravity at long range, and we derive the complete tower of tail terms at this order. The correction arises from the first subleading term in the retardation expansion and is determined by the asymptotic Newtonian trajectories of the massive particles. Its coefficients are again determined entirely by the asymptotic trajectories and are independent of the details of the interaction. For $2\to2$ scattering, the leading-logarithmic series can be summed in closed form. Our results agree with a relativistic conjecture proposed in earlier work arXiv:2610.07134.

Abstract: In this work, we study vector field theories with spontaneous Lorentz violation induced by a nonderivative potential $V(X)$ (with $X = B_μB^μ$), known as bumblebee models, with a Maxwell kinetic term in Minkowski spacetime. We show explicitly that the standard quadratic potential has a Hamiltonian that is unbounded from below and thus unstable. We analyze the Hamiltonian equations of motion for the timelike case and demonstrate that they are regular at the global minimum. Furthermore, we examine the linearized spectrum of small fluctuations around the minimum. We also study particular solutions with spherical symmetry analytically and numerically, without assuming linearization. We find that the time evolution of smooth initial configurations can develop shock-like singularities, and we explain why this behavior is expected in this model. We also identify a subset of the space of field configurations in which the field equations cease to be hyperbolic, leading to a breakdown of well-posedness of the Cauchy initial-value problem.

Abstract: We study the connection between the $SU(N)_{K}$ Wess-Zumino-Witten (WZW) model and $SU(N)_{K'}$ WZW models, enforcing the unbroken $\mathbb{Z}_{N}$ symmetries. The fundamental underlying structures are the (half-)integer spin simple currents in the topological quantum field theories and anomaly matching conditions in conformal field theories. On the other hand, the anomaly matching condition results in the weaker condition $K= \pm RUK' (\text{mod}.N)$ with $\text{gcd}(R,N)=\text{gcd}(U,N)=1$. Surprisingly, the anomaly matching without the pairing condition results in the intrinsic mismatch of the preservation law of the $\mathbb{Z}_{N}$ defects and symmetries. Abstract: We investigate Krylov-space dynamics of primordial perturbations in two-field inflation. We derive the evolution of the squeezing strength and squeezing angle and use them to evaluate the Krylov complexity, Krylov entropy, and channel-resolved Lanczos coefficients. Our numerical results show that the two channels remain weakly squeezed inside the horizon but develop different squeezing histories after horizon exit. The two channels exhibit different onset times and distinct oscillatory Krylov dynamics. The second channel develops strong squeezing earlier and dominates the intermediate evolution, whereas the first channel shows a delayed enhancement. These oscillations originate from the relative phases entering the squeezing equations and are amplified through the nonlinear relation between Krylov complexity of different channels.

Abstract: Finite-$N$ trace relations can produce $Q$-cohomology classes with no stable large-$N$ ancestor. Substitution into $O_{24}$ gives a closed rational operator for each pair. Multiplying by eigenvalue-separation factors removes the denominators, and summing over all pairs produces a gauge-invariant polynomial. An exact block-restriction identity recovers a nonzero multiple of $O_{24}$. The restriction maps every stable class to a stable $SU(2)$ class, proving that the resulting operator is non-exact and fortuitous, even when non-Coulombic stable classes are included. This proves the existence of fortuitous BMN cohomology at every $N\geq3$. The construction is an exact algebraic realization of a nonabelian core with scalar dressing.

Abstract: We study the renormalization of Einstein gravity coupled to a scalar field in the Lagrange multiplier (LM) formulation, where a pair of quantum LM fields, $(λ'_{μν},σ')$, is coupled to the gauge-fixed gravitational and scalar equations of motion. Using the fact that in this approach there are no higher loop corrections, we show that renormalizing the two quantum LM fields at one-loop order absorbs, off-shell, five out of the six independent divergent structures. The remaining off-shell counterterm, proportional to $ (\partial_μ\barφ\,\partial^μ\barφ)^2, $ is nonzero on shell and cannot be absorbed by any LM field redefinition, since such redefinitions generate only counterterms that vanish on shell. In contrast, in pure gravity (no matter), the LM renormalization absorbs all one-loop divergences and no additional counterterms are required. The LM formulation therefore provides a sharp off-shell separation between equation-of-motion divergences, absorbed into LM field renormalizations, and the single irreducible structure.

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 在拓扑非平凡的时空中，通常用于选择Bunch-Davies真空的对称性论证不再适用。本文旨在研究具有环面空间截面的德西特时空中标量场的真空态问题，特别关注拓扑效应如何影响红外扇区和零模贡献，以及这些效应对半经典暴胀膨胀的物理意义，尤其是能否为早期宇宙的加速膨胀提供新的理论解释。

Conclusion: 非平凡拓扑结构(如环面空间截面)通过破坏全局德西特不变性，使量子场论中的零模自由度产生物理可观测效应。这种拓扑诱导的零模贡献可以表现为有效宇宙学常数(P=-ρ),为早期宇宙暴胀提供了不依赖于作用量局域项的新机制。该机制强调了量子态红外结构和时空拓扑在宇宙学中的重要性,并可能对当前宇宙的加速膨胀具有潜在应用价值。

Abstract: When spatial sections of spacetime are topologically nontrivial, the symmetry arguments usually used to choose the Bunch-Davies vacuum are no longer guaranteed to apply. We study this problem for a scalar field on de Sitter spacetime with toroidal spatial sections. The Hadamard conditions, which ensure the same short-distance structure as in flat space, are local and naturally insensitive to the compactification. By contrast, we find that the infrared sector is affected by the toroidal identifications that break global de Sitter invariance, thereby removing the usual argument for discarding zero-mode contributions. We treat the remaining zero-mode freedom as an effective topology-induced source and analyze its effects on an inflationary de Sitter expansion within a semiclassical setting. We show that, for a massless scalar, the zero-mode sector can contain a contribution to the stress-energy tensor with equation of state $P=-ρ$, behaving as a cosmological constant. Thus, the source driving an early-Universe de Sitter phase need not be determined solely by local terms in the action, but can also depend on the infrared structure of the quantum state. Nontrivial topology, as a concrete realization of de Sitter symmetry violation, turns the otherwise-unfixed infrared freedom in vacuum selection to a contribution directly relevant to the semiclassical expansion. This contribution could potentially be applicable to the current accelerated expansion of the Universe.

</details>

Abstract: We derive the ellipsoid partition function identities for the monopole-deformed Kim-Park dualities of 3d $\mathcal{N}=2$ adjoint SQCD with unitary gauge groups. The derivation uses only Aharony and Benini-Benvenuti-Pasquetti dualities as building blocks. By deconfining the adjoint into a $C_p[U(N)]$ quiver tail, we construct explicit duality sequences. Confinement and a rank-reducing duality chain then yield the expected magnetic partition functions, including the singlet contributions. We also present an alternative partition-function derivation of the $C_p[U(N)]$ s-confinement and provide Quiver Lab, a standalone HTML application for implementing and visualizing duality moves on quiver diagrams.

Abstract: Following \cite{Yadav:2024ray} we embark on a further study of the codimension two holography in de Sitter (dS) space. The $d$ dimensional bulk dS space is sliced by two end of the world $(d-1)$ dimensional branes which are bestowed with either Lorentzian dS or Euclidean AdS metric joining at a common $(d-2)$ dimensional space where the dual CFT lives. Through explicit computations of the holographic entanglement entropy, partition function, and Renyi entropy in both slicings for the bulk dS space emphasizing their similarities as well as differences we provide further evidence for this duality. We also make comments on further generalization of the codimension two holography.

</details>

TL;DR: 本文研究四维N=4超Yang-Mills理论中经典BPS上同调类的量子提升问题，发现胶子双线性算符的幂次可产生非库仑单调类，并证明了单圈修正对经典上同调中单调类的湮灭性质，揭示了库仑单调类在有限秩下的存在性。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>

Conclusion: 本文系统阐明了N=4超Yang-Mills理论中经典BPS上同调类量子提升的结构性限制。核心结论是单圈修正层面存在明确的选择规则：经典单调类被Q_1湮灭，而Q_1生成的新类必然为非库仑型。这保证了库仑单调类在有限秩和单圈近似下的稳定性。标量判别式对非库仑类的湮灭性质揭示了类似无毛定理的深层对称性。研究为高秩高次幂情况的fortuitous伙伴构造提供了系统性策略，深化了对超共形场论量子修正结构的理解。

Abstract: We study quantum lifting of classical BPS cohomology classes in four-dimensional $\mathcal N=4$ super-Yang-Mills theory. Certain powers of the gaugino bilinear $\mathcal S=\text{Tr}(λ_+λ_-)$ in orthogonal theories provide non-Coulomb monotones: multi-graviton classes that vanish upon Cartan restriction. For $SO(7)$, we identify $\mathcal S^4$ as a superconformal descendant of a known lifted monotone class, explaining the latter's non-Coulomb character, and explicitly construct a fortuitous lifting partner for $\mathcal S^4$. This construction provides a strategy for finding analogous partners for higher powers at higher ranks. Our main result establishes restrictions on the quantum lifting of classical BPS cohomologies for general gauge groups. We show that the one-loop correction $Q_1$ to the supercharge annihilates every monotone class in classical cohomology, while every non-zero $Q_1$ image is of non-Coulomb type. Thus every non-zero one-loop pairing has a fortuitous source and a non-Coulomb target, so that Coulomb monotones survive at this order at finite rank. In the BMN sector, our results imply that non-Coulomb monotones survive at one loop order as well. A crucial step in the argument shows that every non-Coulomb class, fortuitous or monotone, is annihilated in classical cohomology by a sufficiently high power of a scalar discriminant, which we interpret as a partial no-hair property.

</details>
